# Projects and operating plants

1046 project records (one per named project or plant, schema `schema/project.schema.json`).
Seeded from the technology cards' reference plants; every fact cites the page it was read from.

### Ichthys LNG (ichthys-lng)
Status: **operating** | Country: Australia
LNG plant with gas-phase mercury removal units ahead of acid gas removal. Semi-submersible Central Processing Facility (CPF) plus Floating Production Storage and Offloading (FPSO) vessel for condensate; subsea well development | CPF process topsides (described by Total as one of the largest in the world); onshore two-train LNG plant at Bladin Point, 4.2 Mtpa per train | Subsea wells tied back to CPF; gas exported by 889 km subsea pipeline to Darwin; condensate offloaded from FPSO to carriers | JV operator INPEX with Total; ~75% of EPC contract value was lump-sum (INPEX 2014 annual report, search-verified). Named contractors from search only: McDermott/Heerema subsea flowline, Saipem pipelay, Mitsui/Europipe/Sumitomo/Nippon Steel/Metal One line pipe (Industrial Info, search-verified) - not confirmed by a fetched page. | 260 m (TotalEnergies FID release, fetched) | 8.4 Mtpa LNG at FID (2 x 4.2 Mtpa); 1.6 Mtpa LPG; ~100,000 bbl/d condensate at peak; INPEX currently quotes up to 9.3 Mtpa LNG and 1.65 Mtpa LPG Semi-submersible Central Processing Facility (CPF) plus Floating Production Storage and Offloading (FPSO) vessel for condensate; subsea well development | CPF process topsides (de

### Kasawari gas field development and CCS project (Block SK316, offshore Sarawak) (kasawari-ccs-sarawak)
Status: **operating** | Country: MY
3.3 Mtpa CO2 reinjection — Malaysia's first large-scale CCS project. The Kasawari gas field began production in August 2024; CO2 injection had not started as of April 2026 — PETRONAS said on 1 April 2026 it is looking to bring the first injection forward to as early as 2027, from a previous projection of end-2029/early 2030
Technologies: gas-high-co2-reinjection, ccs-depleted-field-storage

Dated $/kW evidence for simple-cycle turbines and combined-cycle plants. The same technology carries materially different figures depending on dollar year, plant size, output basis (ISO/net) and whether financing is included. All figures preserved in the currency and basis stated by the page.

### Gas-engine / genset installed cost benchmarks for data-centre behind-the-meter power (conflicting figures kept side by side) (gas-engine-genset-installed-cost-benchmarks-for-data-centr)
Status: **operating** | Country: Singapore
Dated installed/package cost evidence for reciprocating gas-engine plants. Figures are deliberately NOT reconciled: scope (equipment, package, package+BOP, EPC installed) and dollar year differ by source. Rabobank and Nodus are analyst estimates; EIA is a government statistical aggregate; HTR is an analyst white paper; WWU/SGN is a consultant study; USP&E is an EPC/vendor planning range.
Technologies: idc-datacentre-power-and-modular-gas-ten-year-retrospective
Recorded gaps: Most OEM data-centre engine order values; Single consistent $/kW definition across all engine benchmarks

JHB1 (150+ MW, 20 data halls, 132 kV, design PUE 1.15) opened 30 July 2024 with one of the world's largest direct-to-chip liquid-cooling deployments and its remaining ~100 MW of phases began construction August 2024 (initial tranche mid-2025); JHB2 (scalable to 270+ MW, 275 kV, announced 12 February 2025) carries USD 2.325 billion of green financing raised July 2026 (largest single-asset data-centre green financing in Malaysia, first IFC financing, 30 lenders); JHB3 (138+ MW) and JHB4 (142+ MW) were announced 30 April 2026 under a MYR 12 billion (USD 3 billion) investment, powered by 275 kV dual feeds from JHB2. JHB1 and JHB2 (420+ MW combined) were reported almost 100% contracted at the JHB3/JHB4 announcement.
Technologies: idc-siting-power-procurement, idc-direct-to-chip-liquid-cooling

## WWT: Wastewater treatment / Effluent treatment (WWT/ETS)

### Changi Water Reclamation Plant, Singapore (changi-water-reclamation-plant)
Status: **operating** | Country: SG
One of Singapore's two used-water reclamation plants serving the NEWater scheme: treats used water with deep-tunnel conveyance (DTSS Phase 1 came online 2019 moving flows underground to Changi) and supplies effluent to NEWater factories; Phase 2 will co-locate with a Tuas biogas energy facility under the integrated Tuas WRP development.
Technologies: wwt-activated-sludge, wwt-uf-pretreatment, wwt-potable-reuse-schemes
Capacity: design treatment capacity:  m3/d (x1000)

### Pantai 2 Sewage Treatment Plant, Kuala Lumpur (pantai-2-stp-kuala-lumpur)
Status: **operating** | Country: MY
SEA's largest underground sewage treatment plant: built by Indah Water Konsortium (IWK) under the Greater KL sewage project, replacing an old open plant with a multi-storey underground facility beneath a public park; serves ~1.4 million population equivalent with a membrane-type advanced process; completed under the 11th Malaysia Plan programme.
Technologies: wwt-membrane-bioreactor, wwt-activated-sludge, wwt-uv-disinfection
Capacity: population equivalent served:  PE

### Pengerang Integrated Complex effluent and utility systems (pengerang-integrated-complex-effluent)
Status: **operating** | Country: MY
The industrial-effluent and water-utility systems of PETRONAS' Refinery and Petrochemical Integrated Development (RAPID) complex at Pengerang: integrated effluent treatment serving the refinery and petrochemical trains, plus supplied industrial water; anchors the WWT sector's industrial-relevance link to the REF sector.
Technologies: ups-produced-water-treatment, wwt-daf, wwt-activated-sludge
Capacity: refinery capacity:  bbl/d

### Tuas Water Reclamation Park, Singapore (tuas-water-reclamation-park)
Status: **under-construction** | Country: SG
Singapore's next-generation co-located water-reclamation and energy facility under DTSS Phase 2: used water from the deep tunnel is treated at Tuas with an integrated used-water treatment, NEWater production and biogas energy recovery complex; designed with membrane bioreactors and up to ~2 MW-scale solar on site. PUB positions Tuas WRP as the world's most energy-efficient WRP concept (pre-cooling of digesters, co-location with IWMF waste-to-energy).
Technologies: wwt-membrane-bioreactor, wwt-sludge-digestion, wwt-potable-reuse-schemes, wwt-uv-disinfection
Capacity: design capacity:  m3/d (x1000)
